{\bf j}_{A,m}=P_{A}({\bf j}_{m})\in J_{A}\otimes\mathbb{Q}
is no longer a null junction.
Nevertheless, as the mismatch of
{\bf j}_{A,m}
from being a physical junction is still determined by the coefficients
(a_{\sigma}^{p},a_{\sigma}^{q})
, we have

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=\frac{P_{A}\left(J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{mag}}\right)}{J_{A}^{\text{mag}}}\,.

(4.18)

Intuitively, this measures the “fractionality” of the
\mathfrak{u}(1)
-charges of all magnetic objects (living in
J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{mag}}
) with respect to the charges of those that are uncharged under any non-Abelian symmetry (and hence live in
J_{A}^{\text{mag}})
.
Note that, since
P_{A}(J_{\text{int}}^{N})=J_{\text{int}}^{N}
, the null junctions do not affect these quotients.

4.2
Duality to heterotic and CHL descriptions

Theories of rank
(2,18)
and
(2,10)
have a dual construction in terms of the heterotic and CHL string, respectively, which provides a crosscheck for the junction description.
In both cases, electrically charged states are identified as elements from a momentum lattice for perturbative string excitations.
For the heterotic string the momentum lattice is the so-called Narain lattice
[
68
]
:

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\text{Narain}}=(-\text{E}_{8})\oplus(-\text{E}_{8})\oplus U\oplus U\,,

(4.19)

with
(-\text{E}_{8})
the negative of the E
8
root lattice and
U
the hyperpolic lattice defined by the bi-linear form

\displaystyle U:\quad\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\,.

(4.20)

The CHL string is determined by the Mikhailov lattice
[
69
]

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\text{Mikhailov}}=(-\text{D}_{8})\oplus U\oplus U=(-\text{E}_{8})\oplus U\oplus U(2)\,,

(4.21)

with the negative Spin
(16)
root lattice
(-\text{D}_{8})
. Here,
U(x)
denotes a lattice of rank two with bi-linear form (
4.20
) multiplied by
x
.

Following the notation of
[
13
]
, we will denote them collectively as
\Lambda_{S}
.
Then, if the duality holds, we expect
\Lambda_{S}=\Lambda_{\text{c}}
.
Equivalently, points in the dual momentum lattice
12
12

12

Note that
\Lambda_{\text{Narain}}^{*}=\Lambda_{\text{Narain}}
is self-dual while
\Lambda_{\text{Mikhailov}}^{*}\neq\Lambda_{\text{Mikhailov}}
is not.
correspond to physical magnetically charged states and therefore must be associated to 5-brane junctions (modulo null junctions) in
\Lambda_{\text{cc}}
.
The non-Abelian gauge factors are then specified by an embedding of the corresponding (negative) root lattice into
\Lambda_{S}
; the coroot lattice then naturally embeds in the dual lattice
\Lambda^{*}_{S}
.
It is worth noting that the computation of the gauge group topologies from this data
[
29
,
52
,
13
]
is in a sense complimentary to the junctions approach outlined above.
While in both scenarios, the setting is fully characterized by the non-Abelian gauge algebras (by specifying either the 7-brane stacks or the embedding of the (co-)root lattices), the gauge group is concisely encoded in the projection of the full physical lattice onto the Abelian junctions (see (
4.14
) and (
4.18
)), the methods in
[
29
,
52
,
13
]
extract the gauge group from the projection onto the (co-)root lattice.

To corroborate the equivalence of the two approaches, we describe in the following the precise identification of the momentum lattices with string junctions on 7-brane configurations with zero or one O7
+
-plane.

Narain lattice from junctions

To construct the Narain lattice (
4.19
) from junctions, it is easiest to find a 7-brane configuration in which the two
(-\text{E}_{8})
factors are manifest via the root junctions on two
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
7-brane stacks, and make use of the fact that the lattice structure does not change as we move 7-branes.
Each of these
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
stacks contains ten 7-branes, leaving a remaining four branes to specify the compact type IIB background.
A convenient configuration of this sort has been presented in Section 7 of
[
70
]
, and takes the form

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{A}^{7}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C})\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}(\mathbf{A}^{7}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C})^{\prime}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{[3,1]}\,,

(4.22)

up to possible
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
conjugations.
In fact, the above configuration has two identical parts, consisting of
\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{A}^{7}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C})\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}
, each having a trivial SL
(2,\mathbb{Z})
monodromy.
13
13

13

Note however that the Spin cover of the monodromy is non-trivial and given by
(-1)^{F}\in\text{Mp}(2,\mathbb{Z})
, see e.g.
[
71
,
72
,
73
]
.

In addition, by pushing the twelve branes onto a single stack, one enhances the symmetry to the so-called double loop algebra
\hat{\mathfrak{e}}_{9}
, whose significance we will explain further in Section
5
.
In an F-theory description, one may interpret this configuration as a stable degeneration limit of the elliptically-fibered K3 into two
dP_{9}
surfaces.

Note that for
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
, whose extended weights (
2.33
) are physical, roots and weights junctions agree, so the span of all physical string prongs on each
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
stack (with decomposition as in (
2.34
)) contains two copies of the
(-\text{E}_{8})
-lattice.
Next we need to find the factor
U\oplus U
in the orthogonal complement of the
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
root lattices.
A convenient set of generators for these two hyperbolic lattices can be expressed as
14
14

14

This is the same result as in
[
70
]
(see their Figure 8).

\displaystyle\begin{split}U:&\quad\big(\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}\,,\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}-\mathbf{x}^{\prime}_{[3,1]}\big)\,,\\
U:&\quad\big(\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}\,,\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}-\mathbf{a}+\mathbf{a}^{\prime}\big)\,.\end{split}

(4.23)

Here
\bm{\delta}_{(r,s)}=\bm{\ell}_{(r,s)}
denotes a
(r,s)
-string loop around one
\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{A}^{7}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C})\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}
configuration, which has no asymptotic charge since this stack has no overall monodromy.
Its orthogonality to the
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
root lattice is evident from the fact that this junction has no prongs on any of the two stacks.
Note that, as is evident from Figure
6
, such a loop automatically encircles the other
\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{A}^{7}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C})\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}
configuration.
Using Hanany–Witten transition one can rewrite them in terms of integer strings ending on the brane constituents in the interior (say, on the left in Figure
6
), e.g.,

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}&=3\bm{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}}_{p}+\bm{\omega}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}}_{q}-\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}\,,\\
\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}&=-\mathbf{a}+\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}}\,,\end{split}

(4.24)

or equivalently for the primed stack.

Figure 6:
String junction lattice for rank
(2,18)
theories.

This accounts for a rank 20 sublattice
(-\text{E}_{8})^{\oplus 2}\oplus U^{\oplus 2}
of the full string junction lattice
J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{el}}
.
The remaining two directions are spanned by the global null junctions
J_{\text{int}}^{N}
, for which the canonical basis is

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}^{N}=-(3\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}}+\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}})+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}-(3\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\mathfrak{e}^{\prime}_{8}}+\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\mathfrak{e}^{\prime}_{8}})+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{\prime}\,,\\
&\bm{\delta}_{(0,1)}^{N}=-\mathbf{a}+10\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}}+3\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}}-3\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}-\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+10\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}^{\prime}}+3\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}^{\prime}}-3\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{\prime}\,.\end{split}

(4.25)

Since these have trivial pairing with all physical junctions, the lattice pairing of the above generators (including the
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
roots and those of the
U
-lattices) desecend, without modification, to the quotient

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\text{c}}=\frac{J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{el}}}{J_{\text{int}}^{N,\text{el}}}=\frac{(-\text{E}_{8})^{\oplus 2}\oplus U^{\oplus 2}\oplus J_{\text{int}}^{N,\text{el}}}{J_{\text{int}}^{N,\text{el}}}\cong\Lambda_{\text{Narain}}\,.

(4.26)

Since there are no O7
+
-planes, the 5-brane junction lattices are the same as their stringy counterparts, so we immediately find

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\text{cc}}=J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{mag}}/J_{\text{int}}^{N,\text{mag}}\cong J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{el}}/J_{\text{int}}^{N,\text{el}}\cong\Lambda_{\text{Narain}}=\Lambda_{\text{Narain}}^{*}\,.

(4.27)

Mikhailov lattice from junctions

The Mikhailov lattice describing the momentum lattice for the 8d CHL string is obtained as follows.
We keep one of the
\hat{\mathfrak{e}}_{9}
configurations unchanged, which still leads to an
(-\text{E}_{8})
factor in the string junction lattice.
On the other side, we remove a
\mathbf{C}
brane from the
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
stack, but add to it the singlet
\mathbf{A}
-brane, which leads to an
\mathfrak{so}_{16}
brane stack, which we then “freeze” into an O7
+
-plane:

\displaystyle\mathbf{A}{\bf E}_{8}\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}=\mathbf{A}(\mathbf{A}^{7}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{C})\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}\rightarrow(\mathbf{A}^{8}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C})\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}\rightarrow{\bf O7}^{+}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}\,.

(4.28)

The resulting complete 7-brane configuration,

\displaystyle{\bf O7}^{+}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}\mathbf{A}^{\prime}(\mathbf{A}^{7}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}^{2})\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{[3,1]}\,,

(4.29)

and is depicted in Figure
7
.

Figure 7:
String junction lattice for rank
(2,10)
theories.

The total rank of the junction lattice is now 14.

Inside the string junction lattice
J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{el}}
of this configuration, we now need to identify
U\oplus U(2)
orthogonal to the
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
roots.
Given the similarities to the rank
(2,18)
configuration, a natural choice for the generators would be a variation of (
4.23
).
While the first set exists also for the frozen configuration, the second
U
-factor has a generator with a single
\mathbf{a}
-prong, which would become part of the O7
+
, and not be physical.
Therefore, the set of generators orthogonal to the E
8
root lattce are

\displaystyle\begin{split}U:&\quad\big(\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}\,,\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}-\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{\prime}\big)\,,\\
U(2):&\quad\big(\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}\,,2\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}+2\bm{\omega}^{\text{O7}^{+}}_{p}-2\mathbf{a}^{\prime}\big)\,,\end{split}

(4.30)

where the second generator of
U(2)
is primitive because of the evenness condition for strings ending on O7
+
.
These have an equivalent representation with

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}&=2\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}+\mathbf{c}-\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}\,,\\
\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}&=-2\bm{\omega}^{\text{O7}^{+}}_{p}-2\bm{\omega}^{\text{O7}^{+}}_{q}+2\mathbf{c}\,.\end{split}

(4.31)

After quotienting out the global null junctions
J_{\text{phys}}^{N,\text{el}}
, with generators

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}^{N}=-2\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}-\mathbf{c}+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}-(3\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\mathfrak{e}^{\prime}_{8}}+\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\mathfrak{e}^{\prime}_{8}})+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{\prime}\,,\\
&\bm{\delta}_{(0,1)}^{N}=4\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}-2\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\text{O7}^{+}}+5\mathbf{c}-3\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}-\mathbf{a}^{\prime}+10\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}^{\prime}}+3\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\mathfrak{e}_{8}^{\prime}}-3\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{\prime}\,,\end{split}

(4.32)

one finds

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\text{c}}\cong(-\text{E}_{8})\oplus U\oplus U(2)=\Lambda_{\text{Mikhailov}}\,.

(4.33)

In this case it is interesting to also analyze the 5-brane junctions that correspond to the dual lattice. Here, it is important to remember that 5-branes have different physicality conditions, which allow for an arbitrary integer number of them to end on the O7
+
-plane.
This does not affect the
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
root junctions, and the
U
-factor in (
4.30
), but does imply that
\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}
in (
4.31
) is no longer a primitive 5-brane junction.
Instead, it is a multiple of

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}=\bm{\delta}_{(\nicefrac{{3}}{{2}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})}=-\bm{\omega}^{\text{O7}^{+}}_{p}-\bm{\omega}^{\text{O7}^{+}}_{q}+\mathbf{c}\,.

(4.34)

This implies that the there is an overlattice of
U(2)
in (
4.30
) inside the physical 5-brane junction lattice, given by

\displaystyle U(\tfrac{1}{2}):\quad\big(\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}\,,\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}+\bm{\omega}^{\text{O7}^{+}}_{p}-\mathbf{a}^{\prime}\big)\,.

(4.35)

Note that the null junction lattice spanned by (
4.32
) remain primitive as a sublattice of
J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{mag}}
.
Therefore one has for the magnetic 5-brane junction lattice

\displaystyle\Lambda_{cc}=(-\text{E}_{8})\oplus U\oplus U(\tfrac{1}{2})=\Lambda_{\text{Mikhailov}}^{*}\,,

(4.36)

which precisely coincides with the dual of the Mikhailov lattice (
4.21
).

4.3
A rank
(2,2)
momentum lattice

8d rank
(2,2)
string vacua have no known constructions as
T^{2}
- or
S^{1}
-reductions of the heterotic or CHL string.
However, using the junctions, we propose an analogue of a momentum lattice description, which can be applied, in particular, to determine the gauge group topologies of these theories.
To this end, we start with a 7-brane configuration with two O7
+
’s, that we obtain from further freezing an
\mathfrak{so}_{16}
stack on the primed side of (
4.22
).
The overall brane configuration is then given by

\displaystyle{\bf O7}^{+}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}{{\bf O7}^{+}}^{\prime}\mathbf{C}^{\prime}\mathbf{X}^{\prime}_{[3,1]}\,,

(4.37)

and is depicted in Figure
8
.

Figure 8:
String junction lattice for rank
(2,2)
theories.

The string junction lattice in this case has rank
6
, whose non-null directions are isomorphic to

\displaystyle U\oplus U(2)\,.

(4.38)

The explicit generators in terms of physical string junctions are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}U:&\quad\big(\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}\,,\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}-\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{\prime}\big)\,,\\
U(2):&\quad\big(\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}\,,2\bm{\delta}_{(3,1)}+2\bm{\omega}^{\text{O7}^{+}}_{p}-2\bm{\omega}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}_{p}\big)\,,\end{split}

(4.39)

where the loop junctions satisfy the same relation as in (
4.31
).
The string null junctions
J_{\text{phys}}^{N,\text{el}}
have generators

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}^{N}=-2\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}-\mathbf{c}+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}-2\bm{\omega}_{p}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}-\mathbf{c}^{\prime}+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{\prime}\,,\\
&\bm{\delta}_{(0,1)}^{N}=4\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}-2\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\text{O7}^{+}}+5\mathbf{c}-3\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}+4\bm{\omega}_{p}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}-2\bm{\omega}_{q}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}+5\mathbf{c}^{\prime}-3\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{\prime}\,.\end{split}

(4.40)

The full physical string junction lattice is therefore
J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{el}}=U\oplus U(2)\oplus J_{\text{phys}}^{N,\text{el}}
.

As for the rank
(10,2)
case above, we find that for 5-branes the
U(2)
turns into a
U(\tfrac{1}{2})
, i.e.,
J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{mag}}=U\oplus U(\tfrac{1}{2})\oplus J_{\text{phys}}^{N,\text{mag}}
.
A novel modification that will affect the gauge group computation is that also the null junctions are refined.
Namely, the generators of
J_{\text{phys}}^{N,\text{mag}}
are

\displaystyle\begin{split}\tfrac{1}{2}(\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(1,0)}+\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(0,1)})=\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})}&=\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}-\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\text{O7}^{+}}+2\mathbf{c}-\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}+\bm{\omega}_{p}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}-\bm{\omega}_{q}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}+2\mathbf{c}^{\prime}-\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{\prime}\,,\\
\tfrac{1}{2}(\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(1,0)}-\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(0,1)})=\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})}&=-3\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}+\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\text{O7}^{+}}-3\mathbf{c}-2\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}-3\bm{\omega}_{p}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}+\bm{\omega}_{q}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}-3\mathbf{c}^{\prime}+2\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{\prime}\,.\end{split}

(4.41)

In summary, after modding out the null junctions, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda_{\text{c}}=U\oplus U(2)\,,\quad\Lambda_{\text{cc}}=U\oplus U(\tfrac{1}{2})=\Lambda_{\text{c}}^{*}\,.

(4.42)

4.4
Examples

Using the techniques outlined in Section
4.1
, we can determine the brane configurations and the resulting gauge group topologies for all 8d
{\cal N}=1
string vacua.
This is done explicitly for all maximally-enhanced cases on each branch of the moduli space, as summarized in Appendix
C
.
In the following we demonstrate the general procedure in specific examples.
For convenience, we focus 8d theories that were discussed in
[
52
,
34
]
from the perspective of the heterotic or CHL momentum lattice.
The generalization to rank
(2,2)
theories is, to our knowledge, the first time in the literature the global gauge group topology has been computed for these string vacua.

4.4.1
A rank
(2,18)
example

The non-Abelian gauge algebra of the model is given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{so}_{20}\oplus\mathfrak{su}_{4}\oplus\mathfrak{su}_{4}\oplus\mathfrak{su}_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{su}_{2}\,,

(4.43)

which can be generated by the following brane configuration:

\displaystyle(\mathbf{A}^{10}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C})\mathbf{N}^{4}\mathbf{X}_{[1,3]}^{4}\mathbf{X}_{[2,5]}^{2}\mathbf{C}^{2}\,.

(4.44)

Note that for a consistent overall monodromy, the
\mathfrak{su}
algebras are not associated to a stack of
\mathbf{A}
-branes, but rather in some
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
conjugated frame.
Accordingly, the associated extended weight junctions summarized in (
2.33
) need to be conjugated, and are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{so}_{20}\quad(\mathbf{A}^{10}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}):&\quad\bm{\omega}_{p}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathbf{b}+\mathbf{c})\,,\quad\bm{\omega}_{q}=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{10}\mathbf{a}_{i}-3\mathbf{b}-2\mathbf{c}\,,\\
\mathfrak{su}_{4}\quad(\mathbf{N}^{4}):&\quad\bm{\omega}_{(0,1)}=\tfrac{1}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbf{n}_{i}\,,\\
\mathfrak{su}_{4}\quad(\mathbf{X}_{[1,3]}^{4}):&\quad\bm{\omega}_{(1,3)}=\tfrac{1}{4}\sum_{i=1}^{4}\mathbf{x}_{[1,3],i}\,,\\
\mathfrak{su}_{2}\quad(\mathbf{X}^{2}_{[2,5]}):&\quad\bm{\omega}_{(2,5)}=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\mathbf{x}_{[2,5],i}\,,\\
\mathfrak{su}_{2}\quad(\mathbf{C}^{2}):&\quad\bm{\omega}_{(1,1)}=\tfrac{1}{2}\sum_{i=1}^{2}\mathbf{c}_{i}\,,\end{split}

(4.45)

where
\bm{\omega}_{(p,q)}
is the extended weight of the corresponding
\mathfrak{su}
-stack with asymptotic
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p\\
q\end{smallmatrix}\right)
-charge.
In terms of these extended weights the two linearly independent integer null junctions are given by

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(1,0)}&=-2\bm{\omega}_{p}-4\bm{\omega}_{(0,1)}+4\bm{\omega}_{(1,3)}-2\bm{\omega}_{(2,5)}+2\bm{\omega}_{(1,1)}\,,\\
\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(0,1)}&=6\bm{\omega}_{p}-2\bm{\omega}_{q}+24\bm{\omega}_{(0,1)}-20\bm{\omega}_{(1,3)}+8\bm{\omega}_{(2,5)}-2\bm{\omega}_{(1,1)}\,.\end{split}

(4.46)

Notice how, in both junctions, the greatest common divisor of the coefficients is 2.
Therefore, the fractional null junctions are generated by

\displaystyle J^{N,\text{mag}}_{\text{frac}}=\Big\{\lambda^{N}_{(1,0)}\,\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(1,0)}+\lambda^{N}_{(0,1)}\,\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(0,1)}\ \Big|\ \lambda^{N}_{(1,0)},\lambda^{N}_{(0,1)}\in\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\Big\}\,,

(4.47)

and the global realization of the non-Abelian gauge group is determined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}=\frac{J^{N}_{\text{frac}}}{J^{N}_{\text{int}}}=\frac{\{\tfrac{x}{2}\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}^{N}\ |\ x\in\mathbb{Z}\}}{(\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}^{N})}\times\frac{\{\tfrac{y}{2}\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}^{N}\ |\ y\in\mathbb{Z}\}}{(\bm{\delta}_{(0,1)}^{N})}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,.

(4.48)

Moreover, the coefficients in front of the extended weights in
\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\delta}^{N}
determine, according to (
2.3
), the generators of
{\cal Z}\subset Z(Spin(20))\times Z(SU(4))^{2}\times Z(SU(2))^{2}
to be

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}^{N}\simeq(1,0;2;2;1;1)\in(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,,\\
&\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\delta}_{(0,1)}^{N}\simeq(1,1;0;2;0;1)\in(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\,.\end{split}

(4.49)

Beyond the non-Abelian gauge factors, the theory contains two gravi-photons generating two
\mathfrak{u}(1)
gauge factors.
These arise from Abelian junctions (
4.7
) that are not null junctions, which for the present model can be easily determined, from (
4.45
), to be linear combinations of

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\bf u}_{1}=4(\bm{\omega}_{(0,1)}+\bm{\omega}_{(1,3)}-\bm{\omega}_{(2,5)}+\bm{\omega}_{(1,1)})\equiv 4{\bf v}_{1}\,,\\
&{\bf u}_{2}=4(\bm{\omega}_{(0,1)}-\bm{\omega}_{q})\equiv 4{\bf v}_{2}\,.\end{split}

(4.50)

{\bf v}_{a}
, while themselves non-physical, can be made physical by adding coweight junctions, so lie in
P_{A}(J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{mag}})
.
At the same time, there are no “finer” coweights to make fractions of
{\bf v}_{a}
physical, so
{\bf v}_{a}
generate
P_{A}(J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{mag}})
.
Then, the Abelian quotient
{\cal Z^{\prime}}
, according to (
4.18
), is

\displaystyle\mathcal{Z}^{\prime}=\frac{P_{A}(J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{mag}})}{J_{A}^{\text{mag}}}=\frac{\{x{\bf v}_{1}\ |\ x\in\mathbb{Z}\}}{({\bf u}_{1})}\times\frac{\{y{\bf v}_{2}\ |\ y\in\mathbb{Z}\}}{({\bf u}_{2})}=\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\,.

(4.51)

The generators of
{\cal Z}^{\prime}
are

\displaystyle\begin{split}&{\bf v}_{1}\simeq(0,0;1;1;1;1\ |\ e^{i\pi/2};1)\in(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times U(1)_{1}\times U(1)_{2}\,,\\
&{\bf v}_{2}\simeq(0,1;1;0;0;0\ |\ 1;e^{i\pi/2})\in(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2})\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times U(1)_{1}\times U(1)_{2}\,,\end{split}

(4.52)

where we have used a vertical line to separate the finite groups from the
U(1)
’s, whose trivial element is
1
.

Consequently, the global form of the gauge group is given by

\frac{\left[\left(Spin(20)\times SU(2)^{2}\times SU(4)^{2}\right)/\left(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\right)\right]\times U(1)^{2}}{\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}}\,,

(4.53)

in perfect agreement with the heterotic analysis in
[
34
]
.

4.4.2
A rank
(2,10)
example

Since the brane configuration above already containes an
\mathfrak{so}_{16}
stack we can simply reinterpret this as an O7
+
-plane, leading to the brane configuration

\displaystyle(\mathbf{A}^{2}{\bf O7}^{+})\mathbf{N}^{4}\mathbf{X}_{[1,3]}^{4}\mathbf{X}_{[2,5]}^{2}\mathbf{C}^{2}\,.

(4.54)

This setup has a non-Abelian gauge algebra given by

\displaystyle\mathfrak{g}=\mathfrak{sp}_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{su}_{4}\oplus\mathfrak{su}_{4}\oplus\mathfrak{su}_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{su}_{2}\,,

(4.55)

leading to a model with rank
(2,10)
dual to a specific CHL background.
Except for the
\mathfrak{sp}
factor, the extended weights are the same as in (
4.45
).
For the
\mathfrak{sp}
algebra one has

\displaystyle\mathfrak{sp}_{2}\quad(\mathbf{A}^{2}{\bf O7}^{+}):\quad\bm{\omega}_{p}=\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}\,,\quad\bm{\omega}_{q}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathbf{a}_{1}+\mathbf{a}_{2})-\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}+\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\text{O7}^{+}}\,.

(4.56)

Formally, the global null junctions
\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(p,q)}
are the same as in (
4.46
), except that the
\bm{\omega}_{p,q}
appearing there are now the extended weight junctions of
\mathfrak{sp}
.
Again, we can divide both by 2, obtaining the fractional 5-brane junctions

\displaystyle J^{N,\text{mag}}_{\text{frac}}=\Big\{\lambda^{N}_{(1,0)}\,\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(1,0)}+\lambda^{N}_{(0,1)}\,\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(0,1)}\ \Big|\ \lambda^{N}_{(1,0)},\lambda^{N}_{(0,1)}\in\tfrac{1}{2}\mathbb{Z}\Big\}\,,

(4.57)

implying
{\cal Z}\cong\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, with generators

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}^{N}\simeq(0;2;2;1;1)\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}=Z(Sp(2)\times SU(4)^{2}\times SU(2)^{2})\,,\\
&\tfrac{1}{2}\bm{\delta}_{(0,1)}^{N}\simeq(1;0;2;0;1)\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}=Z(Sp(2)\times SU(4)^{2}\times SU(2)^{2})\,,\end{split}

(4.58)

where the entry for
\mathbb{Z}_{2}=Z(Sp(2))
is determined only by the coefficient in front of
\bm{\omega}_{q}
, see (
3.21
).

In a similar way, there are two
\mathfrak{u}(1)
generators that formally are the same as in (
4.50
).
Since
({\bf u}_{1},{\bf v}_{1})
have no prongs on the
\mathfrak{sp}
stack, we get a
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
factor in
{\cal Z}^{\prime}
, with generator

\displaystyle{\bf v}_{1}\simeq(0;1;1;1;1\ |\ e^{i\pi/2};1)\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}\times U(1)_{1}\times U(1)_{2}\,.

(4.59)

Moreover, since
{\bf v}_{2}
has an order-4 prong that is not on the O7
+
, physicality conditions do not change the fact that we obtain another
\mathbb{Z}_{4}
factor in
{\cal Z}^{\prime}
, now with generator

\displaystyle{\bf v}_{2}\simeq(1;1;0;0;0\ |\ 1;e^{i\pi/2})\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}\times U(1)_{1}\times U(1)_{2}\,.

(4.60)

To summarize, the global gauge group of this rank
(2,10)
model is

\frac{\left[\left(Sp(2)\times SU(2)^{2}\times SU(4)^{2}\right)/\left(\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\right)\right]\times U(1)^{2}}{\mathbb{Z}_{4}\times\mathbb{Z}_{4}}\,,

(4.61)

agreeing with the CHL result computed in
[
34
]
.

4.4.3
A rank
(2,2)
example

The rank
(2,2)
moduli branch has six special points with non-Abelian symmetry enhancements
[
35
]
.
We have enumerated the 7-brane configurations for all of these, as well as the resulting gauge group topologies in Appendix
C.4
.

It turns out that there is only one whose non-Abelian gauge group is non-simply-connected.
For illustration, we consider this example in more detail.
The brane configuration is

{\bf O7}^{+}{{\bf O7}^{+}}^{\prime}\mathbf{B}^{2}\mathbf{C}^{2}\,,

(4.62)

where the monodromy of the
{{\bf O7}^{+}}^{\prime}
is
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
conjugated to be
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}7&16\\
-4&-9\end{smallmatrix}\right)
.
15
15

15

This can be viewed as freezing both
\mathfrak{so}_{16}
algebras of the rank
(2,18)
configuration
(\mathbf{A}^{8}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C})(\mathbf{X}_{[2,-1]}^{8}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{X}_{[3,-1]})\mathbf{B}^{2}\mathbf{C}^{2}
.

Notice that, since the extended weights of O7
+
generate all
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p\\
q\end{smallmatrix}\right)
-charges, whose parity is invariant under
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
conjugation, we use the canonical basis
\bm{\omega}_{p,q}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}
for the
{{\bf O7}^{+}}^{\prime}
, which have
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
prongs, respectively.

The non-Abelian gauge algebra of (
4.62
) is
\mathfrak{su}_{2}\oplus\mathfrak{su}_{2}
, with extended weights

\displaystyle\begin{split}\mathfrak{su}_{2}\quad(\mathbf{B}^{2}):&\quad\bm{\omega}_{(1,-1)}\equiv\bm{\omega}_{b}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathbf{b}_{1}+\mathbf{b}_{2})\,,\\
\mathfrak{su}_{2}\quad(\mathbf{C}^{2}):&\quad\bm{\omega}_{(1,1)}\equiv\bm{\omega}_{c}=\tfrac{1}{2}(\mathbf{c}_{1}+\mathbf{c}_{2})\,.\end{split}

(4.63)

The physical 5-brane null junctions
J_{\text{phys}}^{N,\text{mag}}
(see (
4.41
)) are then

\displaystyle\begin{split}\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})}&=\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}-\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\text{O7}^{+}}+\bm{\omega}_{p}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}-\bm{\omega}_{q}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}-2\bm{\omega}_{b}\,,\\
\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})}&=-3\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\text{O7}^{+}}+\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\text{O7}^{+}}-7\bm{\omega}_{p}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}+5\bm{\omega}_{q}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}+8\bm{\omega}_{b}+2\bm{\omega}_{c}\,,\end{split}

(4.64)

from which we find that the fractional null junctions
J_{\text{frac}}^{N,\text{mag}}
is generated by

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}\big(\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})}+\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},-\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}})}\big)=\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(\nicefrac{{1}}{{2}},0)}=-\bm{\omega}^{\text{O7}^{+}}_{p}-3\bm{\omega}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}_{p}+2\bm{\omega}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}^{\prime}}_{q}+3\bm{\omega}_{b}+\bm{\omega}_{c}\,.

(4.65)

It corresponds to the generator

\displaystyle(3\ \text{mod 2},1\ \text{mod 2})=(1,1)\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}=Z(SU(2)\times SU(2))

(4.66)

of
{\cal Z}=J_{\text{frac}}^{N,\text{mag}}/J_{\text{phys}}^{N,\text{mag}}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

Together with the null junctions, the Abelian junction lattice
J_{A}^{\text{mag}}
is generated by

\displaystyle{\bf u}_{1}=2{\bf v}_{1}=2\big(-\bm{\omega}_{p}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}}+\bm{\omega}_{q}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}}+\bm{\omega}_{b}\big)\,,\quad{\bf u}_{2}=2{\bf v}_{2}=2\big(-\bm{\omega}_{p}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}}-\bm{\omega}_{q}^{{\text{O7}^{+}}}+\bm{\omega}_{c}\big)\,.

(4.67)

Clearly, this leads to
{\cal Z}^{\prime}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, with generators

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{c}{\bf v}_{1}\simeq(1;0\ |\ -1;1)\\
{\bf v}_{2}\simeq(0;1\ |\ 1;-1)\end{array}\right\}\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times U(1)_{1}\times U(1)_{2}=Z\big(SU(2)_{\mathbf{B}}\times SU(2)_{\mathbf{C}}\times U(1)_{1}\times U(1)_{2}\big)\,.

The full gauge group is thus

\displaystyle\frac{[SU(2)\times SU(2)]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times U(1)^{2}}{\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\,.

(4.70)

5
9d vacua via affine 7-brane stacks

Recently, it was argued that one can recover any 9d
{\cal N}=1
string vacuum with gauge rank
(1,17)
from F-theory on a suitably degenerated K3 geometry that lies at infinite distance in the complex structure moduli space
[
27
,
28
]
.
As shown in these works, such decompactification limits have a particularly convenient description in terms of
[p,q]
-7-branes and junctions that realize affine algebras.
In the following, we demonstrate how the methods from the previous sections naturally apply also to these limiting configurations, and compute the 9d gauge group topologies for rank
(1,17)
vacua.

Since the affinization is characterized entirely by the
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
monodromy, a natural proposition is that these configurations also describe 9d uplifts when we include O7
+
-planes.
Indeed, (after resolving an ambiguity by string dualities) this straightforwardly reproduces the landscape of 9d rank
(1,9)
vacua
[
52
]
, including their global gauge group structures.
Moreover, applying the same reasoning to configurations with two O7
+
’s, we consistently find two branches of 9d rank
(1,1)
vacua
[
54
]
, which are only connected through circle-reductions to 8d.

The key ingredient that enters the description for all ranks are 7-brane stacks realizing an
affine
Lie algebra
\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_{k}
, which we will now briefly recall.

As found in
[
74
,
75
,
70
]
, the
\mathfrak{e}_{n}
and
\tilde{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
algebras can be enhanced to their affine versions, by including a specific 7-brane on top:

\displaystyle\begin{split}\widehat{{\bf E}}_{n\geq 1}=&\underbrace{{\bf A}^{n-1}{\bf B}{\bf C}^{2}}_{{\bf E}_{n}}{\bf X}_{[3,1]}={\bf A}^{n-1}{\bf B}{\bf C}{\bf B}{\bf C}\,,\\
\widehat{\tilde{{\bf E}}}_{n\geq 0}=&\underbrace{{\bf A}^{n}{\bf X}_{[2,-1]}{\bf C}}_{\tilde{\bf E}_{n}}{\bf X}_{[4,1]}\,.\end{split}

(5.1)

Note that for
n\geq 2
, these are equivalent up to 7-brane moves and
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
conjugations
[
75
]
.
It is straightforward to check that, in this
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
-frame, they have monodromy

\displaystyle M(\widehat{\bf E}_{n})=M(\widehat{\tilde{\bf E}}_{n})=\begin{pmatrix}1&9-n\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\,.

(5.2)

The hallmark of these stacks is the existence of a special loop junction
\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}\equiv\bm{\delta}
around them (with no asymptotic charge), satisfying
(\bm{\delta},\bm{\delta})=(\bm{\delta},\bm{\alpha}_{i})=0
, with
\bm{\alpha}_{i}
the root junctions of
{\bf E}_{n}
or
\tilde{\bf E}_{n}
.
By a Hanany–Witten transition, one finds the equivalent presentation
[
70
]

\displaystyle\bm{\delta}_{\widehat{\text{E}}}={\bf x}_{[3,1]}-{\bf b}-{\bf c}_{1}-{\bf c}_{2}={\bf b}_{2}+{\bf c}_{2}-{\bf b}_{1}-{\bf c}_{1}\,,\quad\bm{\delta}_{\widehat{\tilde{\text{E}}}}={\bf x}_{[4,1]}-2{\bf c}-{\bf x}_{[2,-1]}\,.

(5.3)

Representation theoretically,
\bm{\delta}
plays the role of the imaginary root required for the affinization of
\mathfrak{e}_{n}
or
\tilde{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
.
They generate an infinite dimensional Kac–Moody algebra with roots
\{\bm{\alpha}+k\bm{\delta}\,|\,k\in\mathbb{Z}\}
, where
\bm{\alpha}
is any root of
\mathfrak{e}_{n}
or
\tilde{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
.
When we seperate the affinizing
\mathbf{X}
-branes in (
5.1
) from the
\mathfrak{e}_{n}
or
\tilde{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
stacks, these junctions, as strings, give rise to BPS states with masses proportional to
k
.
In the affine limit, we thus obtain an infinite tower of massless BPS states.
Physically, string junctions of these type give rise to an infinite tower of massless BPS states.

A special extension exists for
n=8
.
Here, by adding an
{\bf A}
-brane from the left to the
\widehat{\bf E}_{8}
or
\widehat{\tilde{\bf E}}_{8}
, the monodromy becomes
\mathbb{1}\equiv M(\widehat{\bf E}_{9})=M(\widehat{\tilde{\bf E}}_{9})
.
This would give rise to two independent towers of massless BPS states from loops of
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
and
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}0\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
string junctions, which lead to the double loop enhancement of
\mathfrak{e}_{8}
.
These special enhancements reflect a decompactification to 10d
[
27
,
28
]
.
For discussions of 9d vacua, we will not consider such double loop brane-stacks, but the necessary constituent branes form one half of the rank
(2,18)
configuration (
4.22
) that correspond geometrically to the singular fibers of a
dP_{9}
surface.

Among the various types of infinite distance limits of F-theory compactified on K3 surfaces, those describing decompactification from 8d to 9d are captured by so-called Kulikov models of type III.a
[
27
,
28
]
.
In these geometries, the complex structure moduli have been tuned such that the K3 degenerates into a collection of intersecting elliptic and/or rational surfaces.
While we refer to those references for details, the relevant fact about these deformations is that they correspond to brane motions which generate one or two 7-brane stacks carrying an
\widehat{\mathfrak{e}}_{n}
or
\widehat{\tilde{\mathfrak{e}}}_{n}
algebra (with
n\leq 8
).
The tower of massless states from the imaginary root may then be identified with the momentum states of a Kaluza–Klein (KK) tower on a circle whose size becomes infinite at the infinite distance limit.
In the case with two affine stacks, the individual imaginary root junctions turn out to be identical in the global setting, consistent with having just one KK-tower
[
28
]
.

5.1
Global structure of 9d vacua of rank 17

As for the classification of 8d vacua, one can also categorize all brane configurations with such affine stacks.
Then, if the non-Abelian brane stacks correspond to the algebra
\mathfrak{h}\oplus\widehat{{\cal E}}_{n}
or
\mathfrak{h}\oplus\widehat{{\cal E}}_{n}\oplus\widehat{{\cal E}}_{m}
(where
{\cal E}=\mathfrak{e}
or
\tilde{\mathfrak{e}}
) for some finite semi-simple, simply-laced algebra
\mathfrak{h}
, the associated non-Abelian gauge algebra in 9d is
\mathfrak{h}\oplus{\cal E}_{n}
or
\mathfrak{h}\oplus{\cal E}_{n}\oplus{\cal E}_{m}
, respectively
[
27
,
28
]
.
This reproduces, e.g., all the maximally enhanced non-Abelian algebras (i.e., with rank 17) determined in the dual heterotic frame
[
29
]
.

To also analyze the gauge group topologies in this description, we need to examine the full junction lattice, including the branes away from the affine stack.
An important detail here is that the overall gauge rank reduces by 2 as we decompactify from 8d to 9d, corresponding to the re-interpretation of the KK-states (which become massless) and the decoupling of the winding states (which become infinitely heavy) as we increase the size of the compactification circle.
In the momentum lattice description of the 8d and 9d theories of rank
(2,18)
and
(1,17)
, respective, we have

\displaystyle\Lambda^{\text{het}}_{\text{8d}}=\Lambda^{\text{het}}_{\text{9d}}\oplus U\quad\Rightarrow\quad\Lambda^{\text{het}}_{\text{9d}}\cong\Lambda^{\text{het}}_{\text{8d}}/U\,,

(5.4)

with
U
the rank 2 hyperbolic lattice that is spanned by the KK and winding states.
Since the momentum lattice is equivalently described by junctions
J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{el}}=J_{\text{phys}}^{\text{mag}}\cong\Lambda_{\text{8d}}^{\text{het}}\oplus J_{\text{phys}}^{N}
, with the KK-tower being generated by the junction
\bm{\delta}
, there must exist another non-null junction
\bm{\epsilon}
that generates this
U
factor with
\bm{\delta}
, i.e., satisfying

\displaystyle(\bm{\delta},\bm{\epsilon})=1\,,\quad(\bm{\delta},\bm{\delta})=(\bm{\epsilon},\bm{\epsilon})=(\bm{\delta},{\bf j})=(\bm{\epsilon},{\bf j})=0\,,

(5.5)

for
{\bf j}
any (co-)weight or (co-)root junction, or a non-null generator of the Abelian junctions
J_{A}
.
Such a
\bm{\epsilon}
-junction always exists, but the details depend on the specific configuration.

Since the junction lattice reproduces the 9d momentum lattice, it must also encode the global structure of the gauge group.
In particular, it allows us to use the intuition in terms of fractional null junctions to re-derive the results of
[
29
]
.
Let us demonstrate this for 9d models with maximally enhanced non-Abelian symmetries, for which there are two classes of 8d brane configurations
[
28
]
.

In the first class, the non-Abelian algebra (with the place holder
{\cal E}=\mathfrak{e}
or
\tilde{\mathfrak{e}}
) is

\mathfrak{g}_{\text{8d},\infty}=\mathfrak{su}_{18-m-n}\oplus\widehat{{\cal E}}_{m}\oplus\widehat{{\cal E}}_{n}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mathfrak{g}_{\text{9d}}=\mathfrak{su}_{18-m-n}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{m}\oplus\mathcal{E}_{n},\quad m,n\in\{0,1,3,\dots,8\}\,,

(5.6)

whose brane configurations (together with the
U
-lattice generators) are

\displaystyle\begin{split}m\geq n\geq 1:&\quad\mathbf{A}^{18-m-n}(\overbrace{\mathbf{A}^{m-1}\mathbf{X}_{[n-10,1]}\mathbf{X}_{[n-8,1]}^{2}\mathbf{X}_{[n-6,1]}}^{\widehat{{\bf E}}_{m}})(\overbrace{\mathbf{A}^{n-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}^{2}\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}}^{\widehat{{\bf E}}_{n}})\,,\\
&\quad(\bm{\delta},\bm{\epsilon})=(\bm{\delta}^{R}_{(1,0)},\ \ (n-5)\bm{\delta}^{R}_{(1,0)}+{\bm{\ell}}^{R}_{(0,1)}+\mathbf{x}_{[n-6,1]}-\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]})\,,\end{split}

(5.7a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}m=1,n=0:&\quad\mathbf{A}^{17}(\overbrace{\mathbf{X}_{[10,-1]}\mathbf{X}_{[8,-1]}^{2}\mathbf{X}_{[6,-1]}}^{\widehat{{\bf E}}_{1}})(\overbrace{\mathbf{X}_{[2,-1]}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}_{[4,1]}}^{\widehat{\tilde{{\bf E}}}_{0}})\,,\\
&\quad(\bm{\delta},\bm{\epsilon})=(\bm{\delta}^{R}_{(1,0)},\ \ -17\bm{\omega}_{A}+\mathbf{x}_{[10,-1]}+2\mathbf{x}_{[4,1]}-\mathbf{c})\,,\end{split}

(5.7b)

\displaystyle\begin{split}m>n=0;\ m\neq 1:&\quad\mathbf{A}^{18-m}(\overbrace{\mathbf{A}^{m}\mathbf{X}_{[11,-1]}\mathbf{X}_{[8,-1]}\mathbf{X}_{[5,-1]}}^{\widehat{\tilde{{\bf E}}}_{m\neq 1}})(\overbrace{\mathbf{X}_{[2,-1]}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}_{[4,1]}}^{\widehat{\tilde{\bf E}}_{0}})\,,\\
&\quad(\bm{\delta},\bm{\epsilon})=(\bm{\delta}^{R}_{(1,0)},\ \ -\bm{\delta}^{R}_{(1,0)}+\mathbf{x}_{[5,-1]}-2\mathbf{x}_{[2,-1]}-\mathbf{c})\,.\end{split}

(5.7c)

where
\bm{\delta}^{R}
and
\bm{\ell}^{R}
are loop junction that encircle counterclockwise around the second affine stack.
Note that, because the
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
loop is mutually-local with respect to the
{\bf A}
-branes, it is evident that, by pulling
\bm{\delta}_{(1,0)}
across these, one obtains a loop junction around the other affine stack, showing explicitly that imaginary roots of each affine stack are identical.

The second class has non-Abelian gauge algebras

\mathfrak{g}_{\text{8d},\infty}=\mathfrak{so}_{34-2k}\oplus\widehat{{\cal E}}_{k}\quad\Rightarrow\quad\mathfrak{g}_{\text{9d}}=\mathfrak{so}_{34-2k}\oplus{\cal E}_{k}\,,\quad 0\leq k\leq 8,k\neq 2\,,

(5.8)

whose brane configurations (and
U
-lattice generators) are

\displaystyle\begin{split}k=1,3,\dots,8:&\quad(\overbrace{\mathbf{A}^{17-k}\mathbf{X}_{[k-10,1]}\mathbf{X}_{[k-8,1]}}^{\textbf{D}_{17-k}})\mathbf{X}_{[k-8,1]}(\overbrace{\mathbf{A}^{k-1}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}^{2}\mathbf{X}^{(1)}_{[3,1]}}^{\widehat{\textbf{E}}_{k}})\mathbf{X}^{(2)}_{[3,1]}\,,\\
&\quad(\bm{\delta},\bm{\epsilon})=(\bm{\delta}^{R}_{(1,0)},\ \ \bm{\delta}^{R}_{(1,0)}+\mathbf{x}^{(1)}_{[3,1]}-\mathbf{x}^{(2)}_{[3,1]})\end{split}

(5.9a)

\displaystyle\begin{split}k=0:&\quad(\overbrace{\mathbf{A}^{17}\mathbf{X}_{[10,-1]}\mathbf{X}^{(1)}_{[8,-1]}}^{\textbf{D}_{17}})\mathbf{X}^{(2)}_{[8,-1]}(\overbrace{\mathbf{X}_{[2,-1]}\mathbf{C}\mathbf{X}_{[4,1]}}^{\widehat{\tilde{\textbf{E}}}_{0}})\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}\,,\\
&\quad(\bm{\delta},\bm{\epsilon})=(\bm{\delta}^{R}_{(1,0)},\ \ -2\bm{\delta}^{R}_{(1,0)}+\mathbf{x}^{(2)}_{[8,-1]}-3\mathbf{x}_{[2,-1]}-2\mathbf{x}_{[1,1]})\,,\end{split}

(5.9b)

again, with the imaginary root junction
\bm{\delta}^{R}_{(1,0)}
being the
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)
-loop around the affine stack to the right.

By separating the
{\bf X}
-brane responsible for the affinization from each affine stack, we obtain a genuine 8d configuration.
For these configurations, we can apply the same procedure as in the previous section, and construct the global fractional null junctions that encode to the cocharacters of the 8d gauge symmetry.
Since the affinization (
5.4
) mods out by physical junctions that are orthogonal to the root lattices of the 9d gauge factors, it does not affect the coefficients of global null junctions in front of the extended weights.
Therefore, the fractional null junctions are the same for the 8d configuration as for its affinized version.

As a concrete example, consider (
5.9a
) with
k=7
, which in 9d gives rise to
\mathfrak{g}_{9d}=\mathfrak{so}_{20}\oplus\mathfrak{e}_{7}
.
Separating the singlet brane responsible for the affinization,

\displaystyle(\underbrace{\mathbf{A}^{10}\mathbf{X}_{[-3,1]}\mathbf{X}_{[-1,1]}}_{\textbf{D}_{10}^{\prime}})\,\mathbf{X}_{[-1,1]}\,(\underbrace{\mathbf{A}^{6}\mathbf{B}\mathbf{C}^{2}}_{\textbf{E}_{7}})\,\mathbf{X}^{(1)}_{[3,1]}\,\mathbf{X}^{(2)}_{[3,1]}\ ,

(5.10)

we find the
\mathfrak{so}_{20}
-stack to have monodromy
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-1&6\\
0&-1\end{smallmatrix}\right)
in this
SL(2,\mathbb{Z})
frame, with extended weight junctions
\bm{\omega}^{\prime}_{p,q}
carrying asymptotic
\left(\begin{smallmatrix}p\\
q\end{smallmatrix}\right)
-charges as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\prime}:\,\,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}1\\
0\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,,\qquad\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\prime}:\,\,\left(\begin{smallmatrix}-2\\
1\end{smallmatrix}\right)\,.

(5.11)

The two global null junctions
\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(1,0)}
and
\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(0,1)}
can be then expressed in terms of the extended weights
\bm{\omega}^{\prime}_{p,q}
of
\mathfrak{so}_{20}
and
\bm{\omega}_{p,q}
of
\mathfrak{e}_{7}
as

\displaystyle\begin{split}&\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(1,0)}=-2\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\prime}-\mathbf{x}_{[-1,1]}-5\bm{\omega}_{p}-\bm{\omega}_{q}+\mathbf{x}^{(1)}_{[3,1]}+\mathbf{x}^{(2)}_{[3,1]}\,,\\
&\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(0,1)}=2\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\prime}-2\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\prime}+5\mathbf{x}_{[-1,1]}+17\bm{\omega}_{p}+3\bm{\omega}_{q}+\mathbf{x}^{(1)}_{[3,1]}+\mathbf{x}^{(2)}_{[3,1]}\,.\end{split}

(5.12)

It is easy to see that the fractional null junctions are then multiples of

\displaystyle\tfrac{1}{2}(\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(1,0)}+\bm{\delta}^{N}_{(0,1)})=-\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\prime}+2\mathbf{x}_{[-1,1]}+6\bm{\omega}_{p}+\bm{\omega}_{q}+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{(1)}+\mathbf{x}_{[3,1]}^{(2)}\,.

(5.13)

By (
2.3
), this corresponds to the central element

\displaystyle(0,1;1)\in\mathbb{Z}_{2}^{2}\times\mathbb{Z}_{2}=Z(Spin(20)\times E_{7}),

(5.14)

which leads to the 9d non-Abelian gauge group
[Spin(20)\times E_{7}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}
.

To determine the full gauge group, including the gravi-photon
U(1)
, we must first find the non-null generator of the Abelian junction lattice that is orthogonal to the
U
-lattice spanned by
(\bm{\delta},\bm{\epsilon})
.
In this example, it can be easily determined (by avoiding prongs on the
\mathfrak{e}_{7}
stack or the
\mathbf{X}_{[3,1]}
branes),

\displaystyle{\bf u}=2{\bf v}=2(\bm{\omega}_{p}^{\prime}+\bm{\omega}_{q}^{\prime}-\mathbf{x}_{[-1,1]})\,,

(5.15)

which immediately gives
{\cal Z}^{\prime}=\mathbb{Z}_{2}
, with generator

\displaystyle{\bf v}\simeq(1,1;0\ |\ e^{i\pi})\in Z(Spin(20)\times E_{7}\times U(1))\,.

(5.16)

Therefore, the 9d gauge group is

\displaystyle\frac{[Spin(20)\times E_{7}]/\mathbb{Z}_{2}\times U(1)}{\mathbb{Z}_{2}}\,.

(5.17)

By analogous computations, we compute the non-Abelian gauge groups of all models with maximally enhanced non-Abelian symmetry (summarized in Table
1
), which agree with results from the heterotic picture
[
29
]
.

Non-Abelian gauge group
G_{\text{9d}}
of all maximally-enhanced 9d rank
